<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Joe buys a ticket and experiences $8$ rides during the day. Work out the average cost per ride.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average cost per ride}    &= \dfrac {37.6} {8} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 4.70
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4.70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average cost per ride}    &= \dfrac {37.6} {8} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 4.70
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 4.70$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Rishi pays an additional $\pounds 31$ for a special pass which gives front of line access to all rides throughout the day. 
He experiences $14$ rides. Does the special pass provide value for money? Give reasons for your answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average cost per ride}    &= \dfrac {37.6 + 31} {14} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {68.6} {14} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {686} {14 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {14 \times 49} {14 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {14} \times 49} {\cancel {14} \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {49} {10} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 4.90
\end{aligned}
$

The average cost per ride is more with special pass, so it does not provide the value for money.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

No

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average cost per ride}    &= \dfrac {37.6 + 31} {14} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {68.6} {14} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {686} {14 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {14 \times 49} {14 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {14} \times 49} {\cancel {14} \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {49} {10} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 4.90
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The average cost per ride is more with special pass, so it does not provide the value for money.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>No
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Active Eight had $4$ runners in their team.

Find the mean time for each runner in this team by dividing their total time by $4$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean time}    &= \dfrac {51.2} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {512} {4 \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 128} {4 \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 128} {\cancel 4 \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {128} {10} \\\\
                    &= 12.8 \ \text{s}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12.8 \ \text{s}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean time}    &= \dfrac {51.2} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {512} {4 \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 128} {4 \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 128} {\cancel 4 \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {128} {10} \\\\
                    &= 12.8 \ \text{s}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12.8 \ \text{s}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of people}   &= (4 \times 3) + (5 \times 4 + 2) + (2 \times 4 + 3) + (1 \times 4 + 2) + 9 \\\\
                                &= 12 + 20 + 2 + 8 + 3 + 4 + 2 + 9 \\\\
                                &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>

\begin{aligned}

                                &= 12 + 20 + 2 + 8 + 3 + 4 + 2 + 9 \\\\
                                &= 60
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

$
\begin{aligned}

                                &= \dfrac {12 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$6 \quad 2 \quad 5 \quad 13 \quad 5 \quad 10$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the pages read in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Numbers}              &= 6, \quad 2, \quad 5, \quad 13, \quad 5, \quad 10 \\\\
\text{Ordered}              &= 2, \quad 5, \quad \maroonC{5}, \quad \maroonC{6}, \quad 10, \quad 13 \\\\
\text {Median}              &= \dfrac {5 + 6} {2} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {11} {2} \\\\
                            &= 5.5
\end {aligned}
$


Let's look for the value that occurs the most.

| Number    | Count     |
|:------:   |:-----:    |
|   $2$     |    $1$    |
|   $5$     |    $2$    |
|   $6$     |    $1$    |
|   $10$    |    $1$    |
|   $13$    |    $1$    |


Mode $= 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Median $=$ $5.5$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Mode $=$ $5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>

$

\text {Median}              &= \dfrac {5 + 6} {2} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {11} {2} \\\\
                            &= 5.5
\end {aligned}
$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Two numbers have a mean of $12$ and a range of $6$. 

What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


Let the first number $= a$

Let the second number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {a + b} {2}      &= 12 \\\\
a + b                   &= 12 \times 2 \\\\
a + b                   &= 24 & (1) \\\\
b - a                   &= 6  & (2)
\end {aligned}
$

Let's use elimination to reduce it to just one variable. Add the two equations to eliminate $a$.

$
\begin {aligned}
a + b                   &= 24 \\\\
b - a                   &= 6  \\\\
a + b + b -a            &= 24 + 6 \\\\
2b                      &= 30 \\\\
b                       &= \dfrac {30} {2} \\\\
b                       &= 15
\end {aligned}
$

Let's substitute the value of $b$ in one of the initial equations to find the value of $a$.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b                       &= 24  \\\\
a + 15                      &= 24 \\\\
a                           &= 24 - 15 \\\\
a                           &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>

<emptyline>

As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {a + b} {2}      &= 12 \\\\
a + b                   &= 12 \times 2 \\\\
a + b                   &= 24 & (1) \\\\
b - a                   &= 6  & (2)
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's use elimination to reduce it to just one variable. Add the two equations to eliminate $a$.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a + b                   &= 24 \\\\
b - a                   &= 6  \\\\
a + b + b -a            &= 24 + 6 \\\\
2b                      &= 30 \\\\
b                       &= \dfrac {30} {2} \\\\
b                       &= 15
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's substitute the value of $b$ in one of the initial equations to find the value of $a$.
<emptyline>
$

$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

the mode

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Mode is the most common value.


Lets find out the score that occurs the most.

| Number    | Count     |
|:------:   |:-----:    |
|   $12$    |    $1$    |
|   $15$    |    $1$    |
|   $18$    |    $1$    |
|   $20$    |    $2$    |

Mode $= 20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

the median

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the marks scored in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Marks scored}           &= 18, \quad 20, \quad 15, \quad 12, \quad 20 \\\\
\text{Ordered marks scored}   &= 12, \quad 15, \quad \maroonC{18}, \quad 20, \quad 20 \\\\
\text{Median}                 &= \dfrac {5 + 10} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {15} {2} \\\\
                              &= 7.5
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Marks scored}           &= 18, \quad 20, \quad 15, \quad 12, \quad 20 \\\\
\text{Ordered marks scored}   &= 12, \quad 15, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{18}, \quad 20, \quad 20 \\\\
\text{Median}                 &= \dfrac {5 + 10} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {15} {2} \\\\
                              &= 7.5
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

the mean

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}             &= \dfrac {12 + 15 + 18 + 20 + 20} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {85} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {17 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {17 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
                        &= 17
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17$

</div>

                        &= \dfrac {17 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
                        &= 17
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The cost of a bottle of lemonade in four different shops is

$42 \ \text{p}, 38 \ \text{p}, 40 \ \text{p}$ and $36 \ \text{p}$.

Calculate the mean price of a bottle of lemonade.

![the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2009--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-15-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2009--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-15-subquestion-1-00.png "the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2009--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-15-subquestion-1-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean price}       &= \dfrac {40 + 42 + 38 + 36} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {156} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {39 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {39 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
                        &= 39 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$39 \ \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean price}       &= \dfrac {40 + 42 + 38 + 36} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {156} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {39 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {39 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
                        &= 39 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$39 \ \text{p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Freshly-squeezed lemonade costs $57 \ \text{p}$ a bottle.

How much would $13$ bottles of this lemonade cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 13 bottles}   &= 57 \ \text{p} \times 13 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 0.57 \times 13 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 7.14
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 7.14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 13 bottles}   &= 57 \ \text{p} \times 13 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 0.57 \times 13 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 7.14
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 7.14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

Calculate the mean of these numbers.

$9 \quad 14 \quad 7 \quad 17 \quad 8$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Mean} &= \dfrac {9 + 14 + 7 + 17 + 8} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {55} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {11 \times 5} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
             &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$

\text {Mean} &= \dfrac {9 + 14 + 7 + 17 + 8} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {55} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {11 \times 5} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
             &= 11
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_g2 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>24E2739E-F667-4506-94E5-CE248BC1F021</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2010--group-1:1:35</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tstatistics
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Mrs Fowler keeps chickens.

The table shows how many eggs her chickens laid on each weekday last week.

|Days      |Number of eggs|
|:--------:|:------------:|
|Monday    |$4$           |
|Tuesday   |$?$           |
|Wednesday |$4$           |
|Thursday  |$3$           |
|Friday    |$11$          |

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2010--group-1/section-1-question-35-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2010--group-1/section-1-question-35-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2010--group-1/section-1-question-35-00.png")

The mean number of eggs laid from Monday to Friday last week was $6$.

How many eggs were laid on Tuesday?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the number of eggs laid on Tuesday $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {4 + a + 4 + 3 + 11} {5}         &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac {a + 22} {5}                     &= 6 \\\\
a + 22                                  &= 6 \times 5 \\\\
a + 22                                  &= 30 \\\\
a                                       &= 30 - 22 \\\\
a                                       &= 8
\end {aligned}
$

Eggs laid on Tuesday $= 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the number of eggs laid on Tuesday $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {4 + a + 4 + 3 + 11} {5}         &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac {a + 22} {5}                     &= 6 \\\\
a + 22                                  &= 6 \times 5 \\\\
a + 22                                  &= 30 \\\\
a                                       &= 30 - 22 \\\\
a                                       &= 8
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Eggs laid on Tuesday $= 8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Tom downloads $3$ music tracks.
The length of each track is shown in the table below.



|track    |length                            |
|:-------:|:--------------------------------:|
|track $1$|  $4 \ \text{min} \ 45 \ \text{sec}$|
|track $2$|  $5 \ \text{min} \ 5  \ \text{sec}$|
|track $3$|  $4 \ \text{min} \ 40 \ \text{sec}$|

</div>

<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the total length of the $3$ tracks?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total length}    &= 4 \ \text{min } 45 \ \text{sec} + 5 \ \text{min } 5  \ \text{sec} + 4 \ \text{min } 40 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 4 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 45 \ \text{sec} + 5 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 5 \ \text{sec} + 4 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 40 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 240 + 45 + 300 + 5 + 240 + 40 \\\\
                        &= 870 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{sec}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total length}    &= 4 \ \text{min } 45 \ \text{sec} + 5 \ \text{min } 5  \ \text{sec} + 4 \ \text{min } 40 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 4 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 45 \ \text{sec} + 5 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 5 \ \text{sec} + 4 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 40 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 240 + 45 + 300 + 5 + 240 + 40 \\\\
                        &= 870 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                        &= 14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{sec}$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the mean (average) length of each track?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Using value from the answer above,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean length} &= \dfrac {870} {3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 290} {3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 290} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                    &= 290 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                    &= 4 \ \text{min} \ 50 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

spent practising her violin on $5 \ \text{days}$ last week.

|day        |practice time (in minutes)|
|:---------:|:------------------------:|
|Monday     |$10$                      |
|Tuesday    |$14$                      |
|Wednesday  |$6$                       |
|Thursday   |$10$                      |
|Friday     |$15$                      |

</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total practice time}     &= 10 + 14 + 6 + 10 + 15 \\\\
                                &= 55 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$55 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>

$
\begin {aligned}

                                &= \dfrac {5 \times 11} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 11} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                &= 11 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Vanessa also practised on Saturday.

Her mean practice time for all $6 \ \text{days}$ was $15 \ \text{minutes}$.

Work out for how many minutes Vanessa practised on Saturday.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let number of minutes Vanessa practiced on Saturday $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {10 + 14 + 6 + 10 + 15 + a} {6}          &= 15 \\\\
\dfrac {55 + a} {6}                             &= 15 \\\\
55 + a                                          &= 15 \times 6 \\\\
55 + a                                          &= 90 \\\\
a                                               &= 90 - 55 \\\\
a                                               &= 35 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

Practise on Saturday $= 35 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$35 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>

a                                               &= 35 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

</code></pre>
</div>

$
\begin {aligned}

</div>
<div class='workings'>

$
\begin {aligned}

                    &= \dfrac {135} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 27} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                    &= 27 \ \text{p}
\end {aligned}
$

The time taken to make each model is shown below.

|model| time taken                                |
|:---:|:-----------------------------------------:|
|swan | $5 \ \text{minutes} \ 20 \ \text{seconds}$|
|fish | $4 \ \text{minutes} \ 44 \ \text{seconds}$|
|boat | $2 \ \text{minutes} \ 3 \ \text{seconds}$ |
|horse| $8 \ \text{minutes} \ 17 \ \text{seconds}$|

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the total time that Yoshi takes to make the $4$ origami models.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total time taken}        &= 5 \ \text{min } 20 \ \text{sec} + 4 \ \text{min } 44 \ \text{sec} + 2 \ \text{min } 3 \ \text{sec} + 8 \ \text{min } 17 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                                &= 5 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 20 \ \text{sec} + 4 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 44 \ \text{sec} + 2 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 3 \ \text{sec} + 8 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 17 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                                &= 300 + 20 + 240 + 44 + 120 + 3 + 480 + 17 \\\\
                                &= 1224 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                                &= 20 \ \text{min } 24 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20 \ \text{min } 24 \ \text{sec}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total time taken}        &= 5 \ \text{min } 20 \ \text{sec} + 4 \ \text{min } 44 \ \text{sec} + 2 \ \text{min } 3 \ \text{sec} + 8 \ \text{min } 17 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                                &= 5 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 20 \ \text{sec} + 4 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 44 \ \text{sec} + 2 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 3 \ \text{sec} + 8 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 17 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                                &= 300 + 20 + 240 + 44 + 120 + 3 + 480 + 17 \\\\
                                &= 1224 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                                &= 20 \ \text{min } 24 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the mean (average) time taken to create one origami model?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Average time taken}  &= \dfrac {1224} {4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {4 \times 306} {4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 306} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                            &= 306 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                            &= 5 \ \text{min } 6 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5 \ \text{min } 6 \ \text{sec}$

</div>
</div>

                            &= 5 \ \text{min } 6 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$

<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the range of times that Yoshi takes to make an origami model?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Range}   &= 8 \ \text{min } 17 \ \text{sec} - 2 \ \text{min } 3 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                &= 8 \times 60 \ \text{sec} + 17 \ \text{sec} - (2 \times 60 \ \text{ecs} + 3 \ \text{sec}) \\\\
                &= 480 + 17 - (120 + 3) \\\\
                &= 497 - 123 \\\\
                &= 374 \ \text{sec} \\\\
                &= 6 \ \text{min } 14 \ \text{sec}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

                &= 480 + 17 - (120 + 3) \\\\

<ul class='subsubquestion lower-roman question_type_subsubquestion list_type_lower-roman'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

the median shoe size

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the shoe sizes in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Shoe sizes}             &= 9, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 5, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 3, \quad 4 \\\\
\text{Ordered shoe sizes}     &= 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad \maroonC{3}, \quad \maroonC{4}, \quad 5, \quad 9 \\\\
\text{Median shoe size}       &= \dfrac {3 + 4} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7} {2} \\\\
                              &= 3 \dfrac {1} {2}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \dfrac {1} {2}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>
Let's arrange the shoe sizes in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Shoe sizes}             &= 9, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 5, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 3, \quad 4 \\\\
\text{Ordered shoe sizes}     &= 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{3}, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{4}, \quad 5, \quad 9 \\\\
\text{Median shoe size}       &= \dfrac {3 + 4} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7} {2} \\\\
                              &= 3 \dfrac {1} {2}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3 \dfrac {1} {2}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

the mean shoe size

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean shoe size}   &= \dfrac {1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {(\dfrac {3} {2} + \dfrac {3} {2}) + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 4} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean shoe size}   &= \dfrac {1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {(\dfrac {3} {2} + \dfrac {3} {2}) + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 4} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>
</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Jane has recorded the shoe size of every girl in her class.

Her results are shown in the table below.

|shoe size        |$3$|$4$ |$5$|$6$|
|:---------------:|:-:|:--:|:-:|:-:|
|number of people |$2$|$12$|$7$|$1$|

Jane says “The mode is $12$”.

Annabel says “The mode is $4$”.

Who is correct? Give a reason for your answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Annabel is correct. $4$ is the most common shoe size and hence the mode. $12$ is the count of girls who has that shoe size.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Annabel

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Annabel is correct. $4$ is the most common shoe size and hence the mode. $12$ is the count of girls who has that shoe size.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Janet's marks on five mental arithmetic tests are:

$15 \quad 19 \quad 13 \quad 18 \quad 20$

What is her mean (average) mark?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average marks}            &= \dfrac {15 + 19 + 13 + 18 + 20} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {85} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {17 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                &= 17
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                &= \dfrac {17 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                &= 17
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_g2 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>B031DDB3-A80E-4854-9094-75EEF82ADCDA</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-manchester-grammar-school--11-plus--maths--2010--arithmetic-2:1:11</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tstatistics
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

In a list of numbers:

the **mode** is the number which occurs the most often;

the **median** is the number which is in the middle of the list when the numbers have been put in order;

the **mean** is the sum of all the numbers divided by how many numbers there are in the list.

For example, for the list $1, 4, 7, 1, 2$

mode $= 1$, median $= 2$, mean $= \dfrac{15}{5} = 3$.

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the mode, the median, and the mean for the list $8, 5, 2, 16, 6, 5, 7$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let's use a frequency table to find the mode.

| Number    | Count     |
|:------:   |:-----:    |
|   $2$     |    $1$    |
|   $5$     |    $2$    |
|   $6$     |    $1$    |
|   $7$     |    $1$    |
|   $8$     |    $1$    |
|   $16$    |    $1$    |

Mode $= 5$

Let's arrange the numbers in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Numbers}           &= 8, \quad 5, \quad 2, \quad 16, \quad 6, \quad 5, \quad 7 \\\\
\text{Ordered numbers}   &= 2, \quad 5, \quad 5, \quad \maroonC{6}, \quad 7, \quad 8, \quad 16 \\\\
\text{Median}            &= 6
\end {aligned}
$

Now let's calculate the median for the list.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean}    &= \dfrac {2 + 5 + 5 + 6 + 7 + 8 + 16} {7} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {49} {7} \\\\
                &= 7
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Mode $=$ $5$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Median $=$ $6$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Mean $=$ $7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median

rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean

|   $7$     |    $1$    |
|   $8$     |    $1$    |
|   $16$    |    $1$    |

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down a list of three whole numbers for which the mode is $4$ and the mean is $6$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


The mode is $4$ for the list of three numbers, so at least two numbers will be $4$.

Let the third number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a + 4 + 4} {3}                  &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac {a + 8} {3}                      &= 6 \\\\
a + 8                                   &= 6 \times 3 \\\\
a + 8                                   &= 18 \\\\
a                                       &= 18 - 8 \\\\
a                                       &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Numbers $= 4, 4, 10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4, 4, 10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean

a + 8                                   &= 18 \\\\
a                                       &= 18 - 8 \\\\
a                                       &= 10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Numbers $= 4, 4, 10$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down a list of five whole numbers for which the mode is $9$, the median is $4$, and the mean is $5$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


The mode is $9$ for the list of five numbers, so at least two numbers will be $9$.

The count of numbers in the list is odd and the median is $4$, so at least one numbers will be $4$.

One possible set of five whole numbers is $= 1, 2, 4, 9, 9$.

Let's verify the mean.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}     &= \dfrac {1 + 2 + 4 + 9 + 9} {5} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {25} {5} \\\\
                &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1, 2, 4, 9, 9$

</div>
</div>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

                &= \dfrac {25} {5} \\\\
                &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='papername'>
<p>the-manchester-grammar-school--11-plus--maths--2011--arithmetic-1:1:19</p>

<div class='rag'>

</li>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

Four boys have an average mass of $50 \ \text{kg}$ and 
six girls have an average mass of $40 \ \text{kg}$. What 
is the average mass of the ten children?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average mass}     &= \dfrac {\text{Mass of boys} + \text{Mass of girls}} {\text{Total children}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {4 \times 50 + 6 \times 40} {4 + 6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {200 + 240} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {440} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {44 \cancel{0}} {1 \cancel{0}} \\\\
                        &= 44  \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$44 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average mass}     &= \dfrac {\text{Mass of boys} + \text{Mass of girls}} {\text{Total children}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {4 \times 50 + 6 \times 40} {4 + 6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {200 + 240} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {440} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {44 \cancel{0}} {1 \cancel{0}} \\\\
                        &= 44  \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$44 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

For the first $12$ months after I bought my new phone I downloaded $4$ apps to it each month. Over the next $7$ months I only downloaded $1$ app each month, but this month I downloaded $5$ new apps. What is the average number of apps I have downloaded each month while I have had the phone?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average number of apps}       &= \dfrac { (12 \times 4) + (7 \times 1) + (1 \times 5) } { 12 + 7 + 1 } \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac { 48 + 7 + 5 } { 20 } \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {60} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{20} \times 3} {\cancel{20}} \\\\
                                    &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average number of apps}       &= \dfrac { (12 \times 4) + (7 \times 1) + (1 \times 5) } { 12 + 7 + 1 } \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac { 48 + 7 + 5 } { 20 } \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {60} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{20} \times 3} {\cancel{20}} \\\\
                                    &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='papername'>
<p>the-manchester-grammar-school--11-plus--maths--2013--arithmetic-1:1:15</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='question question'>

On a wet week in Rusholme the mean (or average) rainfall over the five days from Monday to Friday was $12 \ \text{mm}$. 
On Monday, when the rain was heaviest there was $22 \ \text{mm}$, but on Friday there was only $5 \ \text{mm}$ of rain.  

What was the mean rainfall on the other three days?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the average rainfall for other three days $= r$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {22 + 3r + 5} {5}                     &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {27 + 3r} {5}                         &= 12 \\\\
27 + 3r                                      &= 12 \times 5 \\\\
27 + 3r                                      &= 60 \\\\
3r                                           &= 60 - 27 \\\\
3r                                           &= 33 \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {33} {3} \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {11 \times 3} {3} \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
r                                            &= 11 \ \text{mm}
\end{aligned}
$

Average rainfall $= 11 \ \text{mm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11\ \text {mm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the average rainfall for other three days $= r$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {22 + 3r + 5} {5}                     &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {27 + 3r} {5}                         &= 12 \\\\
27 + 3r                                      &= 12 \times 5 \\\\
27 + 3r                                      &= 60 \\\\
3r                                           &= 60 - 27 \\\\
3r                                           &= 33 \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {33} {3} \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {11 \times 3} {3} \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
r                                            &= 11 \ \text{mm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Average rainfall $= 11 \ \text{mm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11\ \text {mm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The mean length of a crocodile is $7.5 \ \text{m}$ and the mean length 
of an alligator is $4 \ \text{m}$. If I have $4$ crocodiles and $6$ alligators 
what would be the mean length of all $10$ creatures together?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean length}      &= \dfrac {4 \times 7.5 + 6 \times 4} {4 + 6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {30 + 24} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54} {10} \\\\
                        &= 5.4  \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5.4 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean length}      &= \dfrac {4 \times 7.5 + 6 \times 4} {4 + 6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {30 + 24} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54} {10} \\\\
                        &= 5.4  \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5.4 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

The **average** height of three boys is $1.29 \ \text{metres}$.  A fourth boy Stephen, whose height is $1.21 \ \text{metres}$, joins the group. Find the new **average** height of the group.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total height of 3 boys}   &= 3 \times 1.29 \\\\
                                &= 3.87 \ \text {m} \\\\
\text{New average height}       &= \dfrac {3.87 + 1.21} {4} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {5.08} {4} \\\\
                                &= 1.27 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.27 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total height of 3 boys}   &= 3 \times 1.29 \\\\
                                &= 3.87 \ \text {m} \\\\
\text{New average height}       &= \dfrac {3.87 + 1.21} {4} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {5.08} {4} \\\\
                                &= 1.27 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1.27 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Two more boys, Richard and Nigel, join the group.  The new **average** height of all six boys in the group is $1.28 \ \text{metres}$. If Richard is $1.26 \ \text {metres}$, how tall is Nigel?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the height of Nigel $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1.29 \times 3 + 1.21 + 1.26 + a} {6}    &= 1.28 \\\\
\dfrac {3.87 + 1.21 + 1.26 + a} {6}             &= 1.28 \\\\
\dfrac {6.34 + a} {6}                           &= 1.28 \\\\
6.34 + a                                        &= 1.28 \times 6 \\\\
6.34 + a                                        &= 7.68 \\\\
a                                               &= 7.68 - 6.34 \\\\
a                                               &= 1.34 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

Height of Nigel $= 1.34 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.34 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the height of Nigel $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

6.34 + a                                        &= 1.28 \times 6 \\\\
6.34 + a                                        &= 7.68 \\\\
a                                               &= 7.68 - 6.34 \\\\
a                                               &= 1.34 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Height of Nigel $= 1.34 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1.34 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

If there are $31$ pupils in Mr Smith's class, how many do not own any of the above animals?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Pupils with no pets}    &= 31 - 4 - 8 - 6 - 3 \\\\
                              &= 31 - 21 \\\\
                              &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of the children with pets, own a dog?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of dog owners}           &= \dfrac {6} {4 + 8 + 6 + 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {6} {21} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {7 \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 3} {7 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2} {7}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {7}$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {7 \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 3} {7 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2} {7}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

When each diagram below is complete the number in the middle of each group of $3$ adjoining cells is the average of its two neighbours. Fill in the missing numbers.

| $5$     |       | $9$     |       |       |
|:-------:|:-----:|:-------:|:-----:|:-----:|

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


| $5$   | $a$   | $9$   |  $b$     |   $c$   |
|:-----:|:-----:|:-----:|:--------:|:-------:|  

Let the missing numbers $= a, b, c$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a                       &= \dfrac {5 + 9} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {14} {2} \\\\
a                       &= 7 \\\\
\dfrac {a + b} {2}      &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac {7 + b} {2}      &= 9 \\\\
7 + b                   &= 9 \times 2 \\\\
7 + b                   &= 18 \\\\
b                       &= 18 - 7 \\\\
b                       &= 11 \\\\
\dfrac {9 + c} {2}      &= b \\\\
\dfrac {9 + c} {2}      &= 11 \\\\
9 + c                   &= 11 \times 2 \\\\
9 + c                   &= 22 \\\\
c                       &= 22 - 9 \\\\
c                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

Missing numbers $= 7, 11, 13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
| $5$   | $a$   | $9$   |  $b$     |   $c$   |
|:-----:|:-----:|:-----:|:--------:|:-------:|

\dfrac {9 + c} {2}      &= 11 \\\\

9 + c                   &= 22 \\\\
c                       &= 22 - 9 \\\\
c                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Missing numbers $= 7, 11, 13$
</code></pre>

<p>3B8ED099-3816-4CA4-A1A7-3046A7BEB6E6</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-queens-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1:1:19</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tstatistics
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

The test scores of five pupils were:

$9,\quad 10,\quad 9,\quad 7,\quad 5$

Whats is the mean (or average) score?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average score}    &= \dfrac {9 + 10 + 9 + 7 + 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {40} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                        &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average score}    &= \dfrac {9 + 10 + 9 + 7 + 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {40} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                        &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

When a sixth pupil sat the test the mean score stayed exactly the same.  

Find the score of the sixth pupil. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the score of sixth pupil $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {9 + 10 + 9 + 7 + 5 + a} {6}             &= 8 \\\\
\dfrac {40 + a} {6}                             &= 8 \\\\
40 + a                                          &= 8 \times 6 \\\\
40 + a                                          &= 48 \\\\
a                                               &= 48 - 40 \\\\
a                                               &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

Score of sixth pupil $= 8$

</div>
<div class='working'>

As the mean did not change after adding the score of sixth pupil, the score for the sixth
pupil must be the same as the mean.

Score of sixth pupil $= 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

\dfrac {40 + a} {6}                             &= 8 \\\\
40 + a                                          &= 8 \times 6 \\\\
40 + a                                          &= 48 \\\\
a                                               &= 48 - 40 \\\\
a                                               &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Score of sixth pupil $= 8$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As the mean did not change after adding the score of sixth pupil, the score for the sixth
pupil must be the same as the mean.
<emptyline>
Score of sixth pupil $= 8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the mean number of goals scored.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}     &= \dfrac {4 + 6 + 1 + 6} {4} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {17} {4} \\\\
                &= 4.25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4.25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>

                &= 4.25
\end{aligned}
$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the median number of goals scored.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the goal scored in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Goal scored}           &= 4, \quad 6, \quad 1, \quad 6 \\\\
\text{Ordered goal scored}   &= 1, \quad \maroonC{4}, \quad \maroonC{6}, \quad 6 \\\\
\text{Median}                &= \dfrac {4 + 6} {2} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {10} {2} \\\\
                             &= 5
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>
Let's arrange the goal scored in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Goal scored}           &= 4, \quad 6, \quad 1, \quad 6 \\\\
\text{Ordered goal scored}   &= 1, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{4}, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{6}, \quad 6 \\\\
\text{Median}                &= \dfrac {4 + 6} {2} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {10} {2} \\\\
                             &= 5
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$
</code></pre>

                                    &= 15 \\\\

                                    &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

Other two numbers $= 5, 8$

<div class='papername'>
<p>woldingham-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1:2:12</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='question question'>

**Rainfall**

The table shows information about the rainfall in two places in South America.

| Place     | Season     |    Mean rainfall                 | Number of months     | Months          |
|:-----:    |:------:    |:----------------------------:    |:----------------:    |:-------------:  |
|   $A$     |   Dry      |   $10\, \text{cm per month}$     |        $8$           |  Jan to Aug     |
|           |   Wet      |   $20\, \text{cm per month}$     |        $4$           | Sept to Dec     |
|   $B$     |    Dry     |   $5\,\text{cm per month}$       |        $10$          | July to Apr     |
|           |    Wet     |   $50\,\text{cm per month}$      |         $2$          | May to June     |


Which of the places has **more rainfall** on average over the whole year? 

Show working to explain your answer.

Tick $(\checkmark )$ your answer.

- $A$

- $B$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean of place A}      &= \dfrac {10 \times 8 + 20 \times 4} {8 + 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {80 + 80} {12} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {160} {12} \\\\
                            &= 13.3 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Mean of place B}      &= \dfrac {5 \times 10 + 50 \times 2} {8 + 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {50 + 100} {12} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {150} {12} \\\\
                            &= 12.5 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Place A has more rainfall on average compared to place B.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

A

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean of place A}      &= \dfrac {10 \times 8 + 20 \times 4} {8 + 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {80 + 80} {12} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {160} {12} \\\\
                            &= 13.3 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Mean of place B}      &= \dfrac {5 \times 10 + 50 \times 2} {8 + 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {50 + 100} {12} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {150} {12} \\\\
                            &= 12.5 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Place A has more rainfall on average compared to place B.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
